Star Wars 50th Anniversary Release Will Include New Music from John Williams

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Paired with a fancy new ‘celebratory’ logo

Star Wars is celebrating its 50th anniversary in 2027. The original cut of the first film (later subtitled Episode IV – A New Hope) will hit theaters in February, and it will be accompanied by brief new music from longtime Star Wars composer John Williams.

The theatrical re-release will add a new logo celebrating the 50th anniversary, accompanied by a score orchestrated by Williams. He orchestrated the music for all nine Skywalker Saga films, helping the series craft an indelible identity. The franchise's music has expanded as other composers have brought their sonic vision to Star Wars, like Ludwig Göransson and his award-winning score for The Mandalorian.

You can hear Williams' new score for the logo in theaters when Star Wars returns Feb. 19, 2027. It will be shown in various formats, including 70 mm IMAX. That means you'll be able to see Han definitely shoot first on the biggest and best screens available.

Williams' Star Wars score will get the spotlight in April 2027 with Star Wars in Concert: The Skywalker Saga at the Hollywood Bowl. C-3PO himself, Anthony Daniels, will host orchestral performances of Williams' scores from all nine Skywalker Saga films.

"We look forward to celebrating with our fans all year long — from the releases of Ahsoka and Star Wars: Starfighter to exclusive merchandise, new publishing stories, unforgettable experiences and interactive games. We are so thankful for our amazing community and hope these experiences bring joy to Star Wars fans everywhere," Lucasfilm co-president Lynwen Brennan said in a news release.

As Brennan mentioned, a new season of Ahsoka will debut on Disney Plus next year while the Ryan Gosling-led Starfighter will hit theaters on May 28, 2027. Disney will also broadcast a Star Wars Super Bowl, release retro toys, stream a documentary The Hero Awakens on Disney Plus, and more to celebrate Star Wars throughout 2027.

From the mind behind, ‘Do or do not. There is no try.’

Eminem Releases ‘Infinite’ EP on Streaming & 30th Anniversary Documentary Surrounding His Debut Album

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

New mixes and additional production have been added to the project, which will be sold as physicals as well.

Long before the days of meeting Dr. Dre, Eminem was inspired by the likes of 2Pac and Nas when recording his debut album, Infinite, which arrived exactly 30 years ago on Thursday (Nov. 12).

Em celebrated the 30th anniversary of his independent release by bringing an EP version of the project to streaming services for the first time, which features six of the LP’s 11 songs: “Infinite,” “It’s O.K.,” “Tonite,” “313,” “Open Mic” and “Never 2 Far.”

Physical editions of Infinite are available at Org Music, with vinyl, CD and cassette options available for pre-order; they will arrive Feb. 26. Jake Bass and Mike “Slim” Wilde provide new mixes and additional production to the project.

Org Music also released a 10-minute documentary celebrating Infinite‘s anniversary, featuring interviews with key players such as the Bass Brothers as well as Eminem, and archival footage from the mid-’90s era surrounding the project. (Watch the short film above.)

“After the Infinite release, we ended up [giving] the rest of the CDs and cassettes that didn’t sell just going to the places we could give ’em out,” Em recalls in the minidoc after selling less than 100 copies in ’96.

Released by WEB Entertainment, the album’s 1996 arrival didn’t bring Eminem the commercial breakthrough and radio success he had originally hoped for. Mark and Jeff of the Bass Brothers took Em under their wing and invested in the Detroit rapper, allowing him and other future members of D12 to record at their Bassmint studio. Mark saw the vision for Eminem, while Jeff just didn’t get it at the time. Infinite‘s production was helmed by Em and Denaun Porter.

However, it all began to change when Em stopped making music seeking validation and dropped his filter to lean into his Slim Shady alter ego heading into 1997’s The Slim Shady EP, which caught the attention of Dr. Dre.

“As soon as I stopped giving a f — k about what I was saying, people started giving a f — k. It was like a reverse effect,” Eminem says in the short film.

The rapper’s mainstream explosion came with The Slim Shady LP in February 1999, which debuted at No. 2 on the Billboard 200, while the Detroit native picked up his first top 40 hit on the Billboard Hot 100 with “My Name Is.”

10 Greatest Cat-and-Mouse Thriller Movies Of All Time, Ranked

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The world of thrillers hinges around cat-and-mouse stories, with plenty of the best thriller movies there are working as well as they do because of the characters they put up against one other in their narrative. Though just about every genre relies on its heroes and villains, thrillers oftentimes tend to do so slightly more, relying on an evenly balanced protagonist and antagonist to make its surprises as potent as possible.

There's something mythic about seeing two people who seem equally capable of taking one another down going toe-to-toe, as the smallest shift in things can thus bring major results in a story, and the matter of who comes out on top is usually not as simple as it might initially appear.

Indeed, cat-and-mouse thrillers have a penchant for throwing all the conventional rules of film stories out of the window, with villains winning, heroes being corrupted and never redeemed, and nobody being safe from a brutal demise. These are story approaches that carry some major risks with them, but plenty of cat-and-mouse thrillers pull all this and more off well, as these ten movies prove.

One of the highest-rated movies in the generally staggeringly well-received roster of films created by the Coen brothers, 1984's Blood Simple is a neo-noir crime thriller that keeps you guessing right up until its end.

Centered around protagonist Ray - who is having an affair with Abby, his boss' wife, leading said boss to hire someone to kill them both - Blood Simple is able to make a seemingly straightforward premise full of suspense. Everyone in Blood Simple seems to be in crosshairs as a result, with the movie's many twists regarding the deaths and seeming deaths of its central cast keeping you on your toes in a major way.

Unlike most of the other major cat-and-mouse movies, The Prestige doesn't focus on the story of a law enforcement officer and a criminal but rather two stage magicians, whose competition to outdo one another becomes increasingly fraught due to the bad blood and history between them.

The Prestige keeps you guessing in more ways than one with this side of its story, as the movie delivers its narrative in a non-linear form. This allows the film to provide you with information exactly as and when it chooses, and means certain narrative events aren't clear far longer than they would be otherwise, which works to leave a lot of questions lingering in the plot.

With that said, the final plot twists of The Prestige are borderline impossible to predict beforehand, and are sold decidedly well by leading stars Hugh Jackman and Christian Bale, allowing the more decidedly larger-than-life elements of the story to take you fully by surprise.

One of the darker cat and mouse movies out there, I Saw The Devil is an action thriller that veers into outright horror movie territory at times. This is due to the bleak way in which it explores its story of a South Korean National Intelligence Service member hunting down a depraved serial killer, and how the matter marks the protagonist increasingly as the story progresses.

Agent Kim Soo-hyeon's investment in taking down Jang Kyung-chul is made personal by the killer being responsible for the brutal death of his fiancé, Jang Joo-yun. This tees up Soo-hyeon to be willing to stop at nothing to find his target, with it being suggested as time goes on that justice, to him, is making Kyung-chul suffer, rather than simply ending his life.

This makes the conflict that the events of I Saw The Devil hinge around all the more tense, as we're left to question what the endgame of this matter will look like, and whether Soo-hyeon's concept of vengeance will come at the cost of his remaning humanity by the time the film is through.

Despite releasing over 30 years ago, Silence of the Lambs is still one of the first films people tend to go to when they think of talented actors such as Anthony Hopkins or Jodie Foster, with a core strength of the movie being how all its central actors deliver their a-game throughout the story.

Of course, the twisting, turning nature of the investigation to catch serial killer Buffalo Bill certainly helps keep things engaging, and doubly so after Foster's Clarice Starling turns to imprisoned cannibal Hannibal Lecter for guidance on how to find him. Thus begins a mystery not unlike if a Sherlock Holmes movie and Saw were combined, with Lecter providing Starling clues regarding the case, forcing the FBI agent to test her training in every sense before she finds her target.

Those who enjoy Silence of the Lambs also have a subsequent range of other solid works related to the film to enjoy if this core premise works for them, from other films in the Hannibal movie roster, to the Hannibal show.

In one of Harrison Ford's most compelling performances, The Fugitive follows the story of Dr. Richard Kimble, who finds himself hunted by Deputy Samuel Gerard as Kimble seeks to clear his own name regarding the murder of his wife Helen.

Kimble's escape from a prison bus does put him on the wrong side of the law, but the circumstances around his escape leave you rooting for him. At the same time, Kimble somewhat lucking out into being able to escape leaves the viewer also painfully aware that the vascular surgeon isn't exactly a hardened criminal, and sets him up to struggle with staying out of Gerard's crosshairs in a way that many other cat-and-mouse protagonists otherwise don't.

With Dr. Kimble being kind-hearted to the point of risking his own capture to save other lives with his medical know-how, The Fugitive is engaging in that it has both Kimble and Gerard working out who may really be behind things in their own ways, as Gerard gets increasing evidence that the doctor may not be the person he initially anticipated.

The fame and esteem Dirty Harry has as a movie hinges upon the fascinating dynamic between Clint Eastwood's titular protagonist, Inspector Harry Callahan, and the unhinged sniper he spends the movie hunting down known as Scorpio.

Dirty Harry is a classic tale of a hero who doesn't play by the rules and ends up being the only person who can catch a blood-chilling killer, though this places the two characters in a complex cat-and-mouse game wherein both figures play both cat and mouse at different points in the story, leaving you genuinely unsure at points as to whether Scorpio may have bested Harry for good, since the villain is a truly wily one.

As a result, Dirty Harry is one of the most lastingly significant crime thriller movies out there still today, and a film that other releases are still constantly being inspired by, making it even more fascinating to go back to now.

Haunting, engrossing, and a love letter to the world of Westerns in an era that often makes them an afterthought when it comes to cinema, No Country For Old Men is a masterclass in how to make distinctive characters that leave you thinking about them long after the movie ends.

No Country For Old Men's cat-and-mouse story is, to some degree, a cat-and-cat-and-mouse story, as sheriff Ed Tom Bell and hitman Anton Chigurh both seek answers around a case involving a huge amount of money that Llewelyn Moss has found, albeit for very different reasons. This leaves you waiting in suspense for who will be able to solve the mystery of sorts first, and who will get to Moss first, since Bell may be able to save Moss from the danger of facing a hardened criminal instead.

No Country For Old Men forgoes many of the conventional directions that other movies might take such a story in, creating a grittier overall result, and one that feels far more realistic even in its most dramatic moments, much to its advantage.

As the first film to feature famous actors Robert De Niro and Al Pacino on-screen side by side, Heat was perhaps bound to be a hit in some ways whatever its story contained. However, the movie didn't rest on its laurels in this regard, instead crafting a cat-and-mouse story for the ages that pits the two iconic acting figures against one another in its story to great effect.

Heat centers around De Niro's LAPD detective Vincent Hanna, and his efforts to detain Pacino's professional thief Neil McCauley, as well as the gang he recruits to work with him. Unbeknownst to McCauley, said roster includes a far more sinister kind of criminal, meaning the story sees both characters confront their own morals as they strive to outsmart one another, and deal with the impact this situation has on their private lives.

The way in which Vincent Hanna and Neil McCauley's characters reflect one another despite their clear differences takes Heat from being decent to being genuinely engaging, as though they're on other sides of the law, the pair form a kind of mutual respect of sorts that muddies the water for each of them, and complicates their efforts to one up the other.

Seven (or Se7en, as it's also sometimes stylized as) is one of the most well-known crime thrillers following a detective chasing down an active serial killer for good reason, with its twists, turns, and murders being just as significant and harrowing now as they were when the film debuted in 1995.

Seven's title comes from its serial killer harming their victims in a way that reflects the religious concept of the seven deadly sins, with the gluttony victim being killed via being overfed, sloth being confined so as to not move, and so on and so forth. As the body count begins to pile up, Detective Lieutenant William Somerset and David Mills become more and more committed to finding the person responsible, especially since the killer begins to actively taunt them and involve them in their wider plan.

All in all, Seven is a consistently tense experience, being almost impossible to predict in its entirety on your first watch, and repeat watches being even more compelling after you find out how Seven ends and who is responsible for these callous misdeeds.

Catch Me If You Can is in many ways the archetypal cat-and-mouse movie, starring Leonardo DiCaprio and Tom Hanks as a talented con artist and the FBI agent painstakingly tracking him down respectively.

DiCaprio's Frank is believable as someone capable of pulling the wool over the eyes of even many sharp figures, but just inexperienced enough and naive enough that you also can believe that Hanks' Carl is capable of taking him on, given the latter's own skillset. Similarly, the stakes of Catch Me If You Can are balanced just right that the matter of who will come out on top is always a matter of some suspense, especially as it's not always clear whether Frank is conning someone or even actively intending to.

Catch Me If You Can has the edge on other cat-and-mouse thriller movies as well because of its timeline, as its story goes far further event-wise than you may expect it to on a first viewing, and provides an overall picture of the central duo and wider cast that leaves you constantly guessing whether there's something more afoot - and, usually, you're at least partially right.

Rayman Fans Really Aren’t Happy With Legends Retold Xbox Skin

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Rayman Legends Retold has revealed an Xbox-exclusive skin based on Rayman 3’s Vortex costume, and it seems to be the straw that broke the camel’s back for fans.

As we get closer to Rayman Legends Retold’s new December release date, Ubisoft has showcased a bunch of new skins that are coming to the remake. The first was an outfit inspired by Clair Obscur: Expedition 33 that all players are able to use, but things got more interesting when a PlayStation-exclusive costume inspired by Astro Bot was revealed.

Ubisoft then made it clear that PlayStation wasn’t the only platform getting a unique skin, as it recently announced a costume inspired by Rayman Raving Rabbids 2 that only Nintendo players can rock. While those two exclusives didn’t cause that much drama, the newly confirmed Xbox skin is where Rayman fans are drawing a line.

Earlier this week, the official Rayman Legends Retold Twitter account shared a trailer confirming that Xbox’s exclusive skin is based on Rayman 3’s Vortex combat fatigues. While the Astro Bot and Rabbid costumes made sense considering they’re inspired by games exclusive to their respective platforms, that’s not the case with Vortex.

Loic Gounon talks to us about Retold’s recent delay, the possibility of revisiting other games, and much more.

Rayman 3: Hoodlum Havoc was released on every console when it launched in 2004, and the HD port from 2012 came to both PS3 and Xbox 360. There’s really no reason for the Vortex skin to be exclusive to Xbox outside of it being green, which the reveal post even seems to suggest is the case by asking if fans “remember the green can”.

To be fair, Rayman 3 HD is most easily playable on Xbox nowadays through backwards compatibility. That’s me really reaching to play devil’s advocate, though.

To make matters even more confusing, Rayman Legends Retold already has two costumes inspired by Rayman 3, so the combat fatigues collection will just feel noticeably incomplete to anyone who’s not playing on Xbox. Fans clearly aren’t happy with the choice, with Redditor AwesomeDude247 calling it an “absolute crime” on the Rayman subreddit.

That doesn’t seem to be a hot take, either, as there are plenty of posts on Twitter calling out the strange choice for an exclusive costume, as well as the whole concept of skins for specific platforms. It’s clearly become a bit of a hot topic in the Rayman community, and I can only imagine it’ll get hotter when the PC exclusive outfit is revealed.

We finally got a chance to play The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time remake and it's still one of the best games ever made.

Shawn Mendes is taking a page out of Macklemore's book ... sharing a lengthy and passionate statement on his Instagram advocating for a free Palestine.

The singer wrote Thursday ... "FREE PALESTINE. There is a genocide happening before our eyes."

Saying that Palestinian children have "suffered enough" from "senseless killing in Gaza," Shawn said he has been working on educating himself to make a difference.

He added ... "Let's normalize speaking up and standing up for those suffering from oppression."

In his caption, Shawn pointed out three organizations his fans could donate to that provide relief to Palestinians.

Shawn's stance on Palestine comes months after the concert controversy heard 'round the world ... with Macklemore taking a firm and public stance on Palestine while opening for Ed Sheeran.

However, the rapper recently revealed that nine different U.S. arenas turned down his "Free Palestine" tour.

The tour -- which saw sold out shows abroad in Dublin, Paris and London -- had apparently secured preliminary dates before the venues rescinded them once they learned it would benefit Palestine.

While the rapper claimed the owners of the arenas were threatened by the words "Free Palestine" ... a MSG Entertainment spokesperson told us that Macklemore is "not an arena level act."

Seahawks sign OL Cordell Volson to their practice squad

📅 2026-11-12  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Seahawks have added a veteran guard to their practice squad.

They announced the signing of Cordell Volson on Thursday. They released safety AJ Finley in a corresponding move.

Volson was a 2022 fourth-round pick in Cincinnati and started 51 of the 53 games he played in his first three seasons. He missed 2025 with a shoulder injury and signed with the Titans this offseason, but failed to make the team out of camp. He also spent time on Green Bay’s practice squad in September.

Volson’s signing comes as the Seahawks wait for Quenton Nelson to make his return from knee surgery. Grey Zabel and Anthony Bradford are the current starters at the guard spots in Seattle.

Duran Duran Drummer Roger Taylor Stepping Down From Band Due to Tinnitus Diagnosis: ‘I Move On With Enormous Pride’

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The timekeeper who has been with the new wave group since 1980 honored by his bandmates, whose drumming they said helped "define" their sound.

Longtime Duran Duran drummer Roger Taylor announced on Thursday (Nov. 12) that he is stepping down from the legendary new wave group after 46 years due to a recent diagnosis of tinnitus. In a statement, Taylor said he’s both retiring from Duran Duran and from playing the drums after keeping time with the act for nearly half a century.

“This is one of the hardest decisions I’ve ever had to make,” Taylor, 66, said in a statement. “Playing drums in this band has been the joy of my life, and I’ll never be able to properly thank the fans for the love they’ve shown us over the years. But the truth is that my tinnitus has been getting steadily worse, and I’ve reached the point where I must now listen to my body and my doctors.”

Taylor said figuring out the timing of his retirement from playing has been challenging, but that he feels he’s going out on top. “We’ve just returned from a hugely successful U.K. and European tour, which followed our second headline show at BST Hyde Park, our induction into the Rock & Roll Hall of Fame, and a return to the studio with Nile Rodgers,” he said. “I couldn’t have asked for a better final chapter, with so many special moments that I’ll treasure forever. I move on with enormous pride, and with nothing but love for [bandmates] Simon [Le Bon], Nick [Rhodes] and John [Taylor].”

According to the Mayo Clinic, tinnitus is a common condition that affects 15%-30% of people and is marked by a persistent ringing or noise inside one or both ears. It is often tied to age-related hearing loss, but can also result from an injury or the kind of regular exposure to loud sounds that are part of live performance and studio recording.

Taylor joined Duran Duran two years after the group was formed in Birmingham, England by original singer/bassist Stephen Duffy, keyboardist Nick Rhodes and guitarist/bassist John Taylor; by 1980, the band’s lineup had switched up to include singer Simon Le Bon, guitarist Andy Taylor and drummer Taylor. The legendary New Romantic group’s early videos for songs including “Planet Earth,” “Girls on Film,” “Hungry Like the Wolf” and “Rio” were staples of then-emerging video outlet MTV and stood out for their lush settings, occasional female nudity and high production values.

Taylor performed with the group from 1979-1985, then again when the original lineup got back together in 2001 through this year. He appeared on their crucial early albums, including 1981’s self-titled debut, 1982’s Rio and 1983’s Seven and the Ragged Tiger. He did not appear on the band’s late 1980s and early 1990s efforts, returning to play on a few tracks on their 1995 covers album Thank You and then officially rejoining in the studio for 2004’s Astronaut and five more LPs since, including 2023’s Danse Macabre.

Bandmates Le Bon, Rhodes and Taylor paid tribute to Taylor in a statement, which read: “Roger is our brother. His drumming helped define the sound of Duran Duran from the very beginning and having him back with us since 2001 has been incredibly important to us all. We completely support his decision and wish him health and happiness. He will always be part of this family, while we continue exploring new and exciting projects with the band.”

At press time the group had not announced Taylor’s replacement.

Eminem’s Debut Release ‘Infinite’ Now Streaming for 30th Anniversary

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Eminem‘s independently released debut album, Infinite, bombed when it came out 30 years ago today. “It sold maybe 70 copies and didn’t get great feedback,” he wrote in his 2008 memoir, The Way I Am. “A lot of people said it sounded like Nas, and he was a heavy influence at the time. But I had an album out, and I could say that.” Since then, the album has taken on a legend of its own, since it caught the ears of Dr. Dre and led to Marshall Mathers’ breakthrough Slim Shady LP. The album has been reissued and bootlegged for years.

Now Infinite is getting another chance as an EP. Jake Bass and Mike “Slim” Wilder have remixed and added additional production to six of the album’s original 11 tracks for what’s now called Infinite EP. Jeff and Mark Bass, who helmed the original release, executive produced the EP, which Denaun Porter produced with Wilder. The EP is now available to stream and physical editions, which will come out on vinyl, CD, and tape on Feb. 26, can be pre-ordered. It’s unclear at the time of publication why only half of the album is being re-released.

Org Music has also produced a 10-minute documentary about the album. Jeff Bass recalls in the film how his brother Mark heard Eminem do an open mic on the radio and called the station to get the rapper to the studio. The brothers recall how Eminem showed up with D12 and started recording. There’s also archival footage of Eminem and stories from others who helped make the record.

In 1999, Eminem told Rolling Stone that the music on Infinite lacked some of the anger and humor that would make him famous on his major label debut for a specific reason. “It was right before my daughter was born, so having a future for her was all I talked about,” he said. “It was way hip-hopped out, like Nas and AZ — that rhyme style that was real in at the time. I’ve always been a smartass comedian, and that’s why it wasn’t a good album. … After that record, every rhyme I wrote got angrier and angrier.” Trending Stories Sade Are Not Attending Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Bob Dylan’s Meeting With Pope Leo: People Fixated on the Hood, But the Hands Spoke Volumes Megan Thee Stallion and Daniel Radcliffe Reunite for Season Two of ‘Reggie Dinkins’

In 2016, the Bass Brothers, Wilder, and others recalled the making of Infinite in a Rolling Stone interview. “He would come in and do a song, and he’d cut the vocal for it,” Wilder said. “He’d live with it overnight, and then he’d call me and say, ‘I’ve got to come in and cut the vocals over.’ He heard something that was wrong. So he would go back in and re-cut the vocal, and no one could hear any difference. He’d hear something that no one else could hear. That, and how raw it was, is what sticks out more than anything.”

Although Eminem has never officially re-released the full album, he did use it as the springboard for an April Fool’s joke in 2024. “The highly-anticipated next studio album goes back to where it all started,” a narrator said in a social media clip teasing Infinite 2. “Infinite but now it’s even more infinite. Out today!”

Ryan Reynolds & Hugh Jackman’s Avengers: Doomsday Roles Clarified In New MCU Update

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Ryan Reynolds and Hugh Jackman are confirmed to play a role in Avengers: Doomsday.

Avengers: Doomsday's cast includes characters from all over the Marvel Cinematic Universe and other places within the larger live-action Marvel multiverse. The latter includes players from Fox's original X-Men movie timeline, such as Patrick Stewart's Charles Xavier, James Marsden's Cyclops, and many more. However, Marvel did not announce Jackman's Wolverine for the Avengers movie despite the fact that he led the R-rated hit Deadpool & Wolverine for the MCU and that he was part of Fox's X-Men franchise. The same rings true for Reynolds' Deadpool despite the fact that Channing Tatum's Gambit is in Doomsday's trailers. Now, both have confirmed Avengers movie roles, but with a twist.

According to the Writers Guild of America directory, Ryan Reynolds and Hugh Jackman are listed for additional literary material in Avengers: Doomsday. They are not the only ones in that category, as even though the Deadpool and Wolverine stars stand as the only actors on the list, names like Spider-Man: Brand New Day director Destin Daniel Cretton, the creator of Loki, Michael Waldron, and more appear on the same list. In full, those credited for additional literary material in Avengers: Doomsday are Stephen McFeely, Waldron, Eric Pearson, Chris McKenna, Erik Sommers, Jeff Loveness, Andrew Lanham, Cretton, Reynolds, and Jackman.

10 Forgettable HBO Miniseries That Are Actually Worth The Binge

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

These 10 HBO miniseries tend to be forgotten about in the glut of amazing limited shows the network has made, but on review, they're much more watchable than they're given credit for. HBO still holds the title as the go-to streamer for prestige television and shows you won't regret watching till the end.

Still, that doesn't mean every HBO show is a hit. Even their vaunted collection of miniseries has many titles that received the critical and audience scores you might associate with other, less rigorous networks and streamers. The difference is that a forgotten, underappreciated miniseries on HBO is still very much worth your time.

Elizabeth I is a two-part co-production between HBO and Channel 4, directed by Tom Hooper of The King's Speech fame, and starring Helen Mirren as the titular Queen Elizabeth I of Britain, who ruled from 1558 to 1603. The series covers the back half of her reign, which is noted in part for her refusal to take a husband and bear any children.

The series focuses first on her relationship with the Earl of Leicester (Jeremy Irons), and the second on her relationship with the Earl of Essex (Hugh Dancy). Elizabeth I is totally Mirren's show. She disappears into the Queen and turns every piece of dialogue or look of melancholy into something amazing and forceful.

House of Saddam is a biographical docudrama that depicts the rise and fall of Saddam Hussein, the Iraqi dictator who was executed for crimes against humanity in 2006. The series begins in 1979, shortly after the Iranian Revolution, and ends with his death by hanging.

The series covers a huge portion of Hussein's life, and at times, it feels like we're only getting a surface-level story. Still, the parts we do get to see are incredible and shocking. Yigal Naor's portrayal of the infamous figure is amazing, but there's nuance to it as well that keeps you engaged with the character.

Jude Law stars in The Young Pope, a Sky Atlantic, HBO, and Canal+ production that follows Cardinal Lenny Belardo (Law), a New Yorker, who is suddenly elevated to the position of pope. Now styled as Pope Pius XIII, Lenny starts making drastic changes in the Vatican and within the Catholic Church.

His efforts to change the perception of Catholicism, and possibly reinstitute the Papal States, serve as the backdrop for an overly melodramatic but surprisingly funny series that often has its tongue in its cheek. The production design and costuming alone are reasons to watch the series.

Gunpowder is based on the 1605 Gunpowder Plot in London, when English Catholics attempted to assassinate the Protestant King James VI of Scotland and I of England by blowing up the House of Lords. Though Guy Fawkes (Tom Cullen) is most associated with the conspiracy, it was led by Robert Catesby (Kit Harrington).

The series is remembered for some explicit and difficult-to-watch instances of torture. Accurate or not, they suggest a grimmer tone than the series is actually operating in, which is much more of a spy thriller. If you can stomach those scenes, or fast-forward through them, you'll find a compelling and well-acted historical action drama.

Steven Soderbergh loves to get creative with the film form. In 2024's Presence, he filmed a horror story entirely from the point-of-view of the ghost. In Ocean's Twelve, he had Julia Roberts play Tess Ocean play the real-life Julia Roberts. He's happy to go avant-garde, and in Mosaic, he tries out an interactive dimension in filmmaking.

This six-episode murder mystery was released as a drama series on HBO, and as a mobile app, which allowed viewers to see the same scene from multiple viewpoints, look through clues, and engage with the story in a way more akin to a murder-mystery dinner party than a miniseries.

I Know This Much Is True is based on Wally Lamb's 1998 novel of the same name and follows twin brothers, Dominick and Thomas Birdsey, both played by Mark Ruffalo, which required him to film his scenes twice, once as Dominick, and then again six weeks later and 30 pounds heavier as Thomas (via Variety).

Medieval settings can make for interesting stories whether it's Viking Europe or Kublai Khan's Asia. Here are the best medieval TV shows available.

In 1990s Connecticut, Thomas Birdsey has paranoid schizophrenia but manages his symptoms with medication. However, occasional episodes result in events like Thomas cutting off his own hand, requiring the intervention of his brother. It's dark and difficult, with not much of a light at the end of the tunnel, but it's powerful for it.

Philip Roth's 2004 novel of the same name serves as the inspiration for another Ed Burns and David Simon HBO TV show. The Plot Against America is set in an alternate history where Charles Lindbergh, a real-life aviator and noted xenophobic populist, is elected president, shaping America.

In this new world, America has pivoted towards fascism in the '40s. The election of the popular celebrity is met with elation by some, and quiet discomfort by others. What begins with a neutral stance on the Nazis ends with rampant antisemitic violence, martial law, and the collapse of the old America. It's a troubling series that hits painfully close to home.

Luca Guadagnino's We Are Who We Are is a coming-of-age story set on a fictional US military base in Chioggia, Italy, where 14-year-old Fraser Wilson (Jack Dylan Grazer) has just moved to from New York City. There, he meets Caitlin Poythres (Jordan Kristine Seamón), who has been on the base for years and is beginning to question her gender identity.

The picturesque Italian coastline provides the backdrop for an achingly modern series that takes on some of the stickiest modern cultural and political topics. As Guadagnino is wont to do, he never shows his cards all at once, preferring to take on these questions from multiple angles.

Woody Harrelson and Justin Theroux star in White House Plumbers as E. Howard Hunt and G. Gordon Liddy, the two men who organized and directed the break-in at the Watergate building which led to the Watergate scandal, which ended in Richard Nixon's resignation.

The series does its best to stick to the history of what really happened in the events leading up to the break-in and the fallout, and by doing so, White House Plumbers does feel a bit overstuffed. Even so, Theorux and Harrelson's chemistry and careful sense of tone make this a historical drama that's a blast to watch.

Kate Winslet's The Regime is a black comedy set in a fictional, unnamed, Central European country. Chancellor Elena Vernham (Winslet) is the vain and corrupt dictator of the country, but in recent years, her government has begun to crumble thanks to her laws and impositions on the country.

Increasingly paranoid, Elena attempts to expand her power further, only hastening her downfall. The Regime tries for some sharp-tongued satire that doesn't always hit, but is more than made up for with Winslet's performance. Her bold decisions make this an HBO miniseries that feels unique among the ones she usually appears in.

Removed From 2,200 Theaters, Brad Pitt’s Must-Watch Survival Thriller Officially Comes Home

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Brad Pitt is entering a new stage of his career, but he isn’t slowing down as he continues to seek awards glory. Last year, Pitt teamed up with Top Gun: Maverick director Joseph Kosinski for what would go on to become the highest-grossing movie of his entire career, F1: The Movie. The film has been in the Apple TV top 10 for over 300 days now, since being added to the platform at the end of last year after wrapping up its theatrical run. Later this month, Pitt will reunite with Quentin Tarantino for the sequel to his only Oscar-winning performance in Once Upon a Time… in Hollywood. The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth (directed by David Fincher) lands in IMAX this month before it begins streaming exclusively on Netflix in December.

However, between F1 and The Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth, Brad Pitt reunited with director David Ayer for the new survival thriller, Heart of the Beast. Pitt and Ayer previously worked together on Fury, the 2010s WWII movie also featuring an unforgettable performance from Jon Bernthal. After getting off to a slow start at the box office, solid word-of-mouth marketing helped Heart of the Beast reach $188 million at the global box office, which is nearly enough to avoid being branded a flop, thanks to its $82 million budget. After recently being removed from over 2,000 theaters, now that it’s at the end of its life cycle, Heart of the Beast is officially available to watch at home. Fans should have no trouble finding the movie on the VOD platform of their choice, such as Prime Video, Apple TV, or even Fandango at Home.

Cameron Alexander wrote the script for Heart of the Beast, and despite rumors to the contrary, the film is not based on a true story. In addition to Brad Pitt, the film also stars J.K. Simmons, the Oscar-winner who is best known for his roles in the Spider-Man and Invincible universes. The official synopsis for Heart of the Beast, which holds scores of 84% from critics and 94% from audiences on Rotten Tomatoes, reads as follows:

“After a harrowing plane crash, Special Forces veteran James Belmont (Brad Pitt) and his combat dog, Odin, find themselves stranded deep in the Alaskan wilderness. Together, they are forced into a brutal fight for survival against the elements. From acclaimed filmmaker David Ayer, Heart of the Beast is an intense adventure thriller that explores the unbreakable bond between a man and his best friend as they face their greatest battle yet.”

Karin Slaughter Said “Absolutely Not” to Adapting Her Novel ‘The Good Daughter.’ Then She Wrote the Whole Series

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The bestselling novelist initially turned down Bruna Papandrea, wrote all six episodes of the Peacock series on her own and only then pitched it: "I wanted to make sure I could land the plane."

Some stories refuse to stay on the page. The Hollywood Reporter’s Beyond the Book column explores what happens when books make the leap to screen and beyond — unpacking what stayed, changed, how it was done and why it matters with the creatives who made it.

Karin Slaughter never planned to adapt The Good Daughter.

When producer Bruna Papandrea asked whether she’d be interested in bringing one of her novels to the screen herself, the author’s answer was immediate. “I said, ‘Absolutely not,’” Slaughter tells The Hollywood Reporter. “I don’t have time.” Related Stories TV 'The Good Daughter' Review: Rose Byrne and Meghann Fahy Elevate Peacock's Overly Familiar Southern-Fried Thriller General News Foundation Books Unveils First Titles in Macmillan Partnership; John Goodman to Narrate Animated Short Adaptation (Exclusive)

She’d already watched two of her books become hits in other hands: Netflix’s Pieces of Her, adapted by Charlotte Stoudt, and ABC’s Will Trent, run by Liz Heldens. Though she declined, Slaughter couldn‘t move on, and the idea that she might do it herself stuck. So she started writing the scripts, and she told no one.

“I wrote them completely on my own because I didn’t want to tell anybody if I’d done a bad job,” she tells THR. When she finally told her agent, she made one request: Don’t tell Papandrea — or anyone for that matter — yet. “Because she’d want an option. I just wanted to do this in secret.”

The secrecy also gave her time to take on the creative challenge. “I know from my novel writing that the beginning of a story is important, but just as important is the end,” she says. “People can read a book they think is mediocre, and that’s fine. But if it starts out really great and craps out at the end, they’re furious. So I wanted to make sure I could land the plane.”

With the scripts done, Slaughter went back to Papandrea. “I said to Bruna, ‘This is the show. If you’re interested, that’s great. If not, obviously I’ll take notes, but I haven’t wasted your time and money on an option.’ She read it overnight and said, ‘Let’s go, let’s do this.’”

The result is The Good Daughter, Peacock’s six-episode adaptation of Slaughter’s 2017 bestseller, which drops on the streamer in full on Nov. 12. Slaughter is the sole writer on every episode and serves as showrunner and executive producer.

The series stars Rose Byrne and Meghann Fahy as Samantha and Charlotte Quinn, sisters still living with the aftermath of a single event of violence during their childhood, with Brendan Gleeson as their father, Rusty. The cast also includes Shayna Benardo, Olivia Williams, Harper Steele, Jake McDorman and Michael Dorman. Made Up Stories, Papandrea’s banner, produces with Fifth Season.

When the team took the project to streamers, Peacock “just got it,” Slaughter says. “They had thoughtful questions, and they seemed like people who would be good partners.”

Though an author taking on the showrunner role for the first time, Slaughter‘s résumé shows why she could afford to skip the usual option-and-develop process. Will Trent was renewed by ABC this spring for a fifth season and had 10 million cross-platform viewers last season, making Slaughter’s catalog one of the more reliable book-to-TV pipelines. The Good Daughter, though, is the first time she’s been in charge of that pipeline herself, and she set ground rules early. Drawing on her long relationship with her book editor, she made a request of the network about notes.

“I said, ‘Obviously there are going to be notes, but I need you to speak with one voice,’” she recalls. “You can have all the meetings in the world and discuss it, but I don’t want a note that Kevin from accounting suggested and you don’t agree with.”

She also wanted the reasoning behind each note on the table, so she could interrogate it. “I’m not a ‘note behind the note’ person — I want to know the note. So let’s have a conversation so I can figure out what you want, how to do it and how to make sure we’re both happy with the outcome,” she says.

As a first-time showrunner, the biggest adjustment, she says, was losing a novelist’s room to explain things. “With a book, I get 500 pages to tell you what people are thinking,” she says. “So it was figuring out how to convey information without making it clunky. I never wanted anything to feel written. As a novelist, dialogue is probably one of my strengths, so it felt natural.”

To keep the mystery working and span across six hours, she made clue sheets as her way “to figure out how to make the puzzle work and give all the pieces.” When a note came in on a script, she could show exactly where a detail paid off. “I’d say, ‘We need this piece of information because it pays off in [episode] five or six,’” she says. “After a while, you develop a certain level of trust.”

She also kept the season concise. “I learned very quickly that as much plot as a novel has, there’s not enough for eight episodes. I made the decision early on: This is only going to be six episodes, because I’m not going to do a filler episode,” she says. “Pacing is a hallmark of my writing. I wanted you to be emotionally invested and on the edge of your seat. There’s the tension you get from the plot, but this is a story that relies on emotional tension, and I needed those emotions to hit at the right times.”

She also pushed for Peacock to release every episode at once. “I was scared they’d say no and parcel it out.”

The reasoning for the immediate rollout is because Slaughter thought of the series as “a six-hour movie.” That interpretation also shaped the choice of director Steph Green (The Americans), who directs all six episodes. “She understood that it was a movie and not standalone episodes,” Slaughter says. Each installment has its own genre — one is a legal drama, another a family drama then a murder mystery — but Green kept a “symmetry of place, setting and atmosphere” across all of them, Slaughter explains.

“I think the first thing I said to her was, ‘I’m so tired of thrillers where everything is dark, even in the daytime. Does anyone have a lamp in this house? I want the bad things to happen in daylight,’” Slaughter says. “That’s what we focused on, and our cinematographer, Zoë White, totally got it.”

Slaughter also fought to film in Georgia. “They made us scout in Toronto, and I was such a butt the whole time,” she says laughing. Once the team reached North Georgia, she recalls showing them the red clay — red is a key color in the story — and the kudzu. “Because we’re writing about things that happen to women and girls every single day, we needed to ground this in a naturalistic environment,” she says.

A Georgia native herself, Slaughter took the story‘s Southern backdrop seriously. “I was very clear: This is not Southern Gothic. Those words have meaning. This is not grotesque, it is not horror. It’s a story set in the South, but it’s not a Gothic story. Steph put together this beautiful look book, and it felt like a perfect fit because she got what I wanted to do and how I wanted it to look.”

The same thinking shaped the score. Composer Jonathan Sanford grew up in Maryland’s Prince George’s County but has family roots in Georgia, and he understood, Slaughter says, “that there’s a way to tell Southern stories that’s just such a trope,” with slide guitar and banjo.

“I said, ‘I don’t want to hear any of that. I want a new, crisp Southern musical language developed for this,’” she says. “As a Southerner, it was very important to me to represent the South for what it is, not all these stereotypes.”

Slaughter teases that Fahy even sings a track that will be featured on the show‘s soundtrack. “I knew she was a singer — she has a beautiful voice — and from the moment we started shooting I was working on everybody: ‘We’ve got to get Meghann to do something,’” she says. “I think they did it just to shut me up. But she did amazingly well. It’s a beautiful cover.”

Fahy and Bryne may star as the leading protagonists, but Jessica Biel was originally attached to star and executive produce the series, which scored a straight-to-series order at Peacock in March 2024. She eventually left the project in September of that year. Slaughter holds no grudge. Biel, instead, went on to star in The Better Sister, based on the novel by Slaughter’s friend and fellow author Alafair Burke. “I loved watching it. What do I have to be angry about?” she says.

Slaughter credits Bryne and Fahy for evolving her characters with Byrne’s casting as Sam inspiring a change in the character. “Sam in the book is a lot colder than Sam in the show, and that’s Rose, because she has this natural warmth,” Slaughter says. After watching dailies, she reworked lines to “lean into and support what she was doing.”

Fahy’s Charlie called for rewrites, too, particularly in her romance with Ben, played by Dorman. “In the book, Charlie is much more thirsty for Ben — she’s chasing him. Meghann Fahy is not thirsty,” Slaughter says. “So I had to find ways to express that longing for Ben in a way that was believable.”

And because Fahy handled Slaughter’s Southern rhythms so easily, Slaughter kept writing her more lines. “I’m going to write the hell out of this scene,” she remembers thinking. “It made more work for her.”

Slaughter has been candid about using violence in her books to open up a dialogue. With The Good Daughter, she hopes the series grounds the conversation in the fact that violent events happen to “real people” and that “there are real people living their lives after these things happen.”

She points to Bryne‘s Sam, who lives with lasting injuries from the shooting she experienced at a young age, including impaired vision and walking with a cane. “I watch a lot of television, and I can’t think of many shows where you see a survivor of gun violence and what their life is like,” she says. “We always hear that horrible statistic — ‘this many people died and this many survived‘ — and we think, ‘Thank God they survived.’ But we don’t think about what that recovery looks like. I wanted to show that.”

Byrne, she says, did “so much work with a physical therapist, figuring out how to move in a body like that. She has such physicality, and to distill all of that into this person recovering from a gunshot wound shows the range of her abilities. The fact that she could elevate that performance is remarkable.”

Despite taking on the showrunner title, Slaughter’s work as a novelist never stopped. After finishing the show’s scripts, she went back to work on her most recently published book, We Are All Guilty Here, and noticed the show has already changed how she writes, no longer keeping characters in one place for long stretches.

“When I write my Will Trent novels, I can have a whole scene in a car, and it’s a chapter,” she says. “But with Guilty, I was moving people around more.”

The reason was a day on the Good Daughter set. “You don’t know guilt until you’ve put Rose Byrne and Meghann Fahy in a car on a trailer in South Georgia, 10,000 degrees in August, and the air-conditioning’s not on,” she says laughing. “I was like, ‘Oh my God, why couldn’t I have set this in an office? Why are they sweating?’”

Whether she’ll take another book to the screen herself, Slaughter isn’t saying yet. For now, she’s finishing her next novel and focused on getting viewers to The Good Daughter. “It’s exactly the kind of show I’d love to watch,” she says. “I’m so proud of it!“

The Good Daughter is available to stream on Peacock.

Mysterious Bandai Namco Trademark Has People Talking About a New Fighting Game

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Hmmm, something tells me this is only going to lead to disappointment...

Earlier this week, Bandai Namco registered a video game trademark for 'FATAL ICHOR'. And as is so often the case with trademarks, a complete lack of context means that it could relate to anything.

But the current speculation is that this ties into a new fighting game that's yet to be announced. That's because Bandai Namco's been hiring for such a project since earlier this year — and the job listings have all referenced some kind of "fighting" or "action fighting" title.

And of course, people are getting ahead of themselves already. There are a few suggestions that this could have something to do with a new SoulCalibur, for example, but I think that seems well wide of the mark.

Even if SoulCalibur was destined to make a return, 'Fatal Ichor' just doesn't sound like it belongs — surely SoulCalibur 7 is the logical choice.

And it's obviously far too early to start talking about Tekken 8's successor, which will inevitably be named Tekken 9 anyway (unless we somehow get Tekken Tag Tournament 3 first).

At least to me, Fatal Ichor has an anime ring to it; like an expansion for Code Vein 2, or something to do with Sword Art Online — or even the upcoming Gundam Rogue Orbit.

Indeed, this trademark doesn't necessarily have to correlate with the aforementioned, unannounced fighting game project — it's just that the timing adds up.

So, in conclusion, I wouldn't get too excited over this one. At least, not yet. We'll have to wait and see whether 'Fatal Ichor' shows up over the coming months; if it doesn't, then maybe there really is a connection here.

‘El Mal Padre,’ Inspired by ‘a Fear of Facing Fatherhood,’ Unpacked by Director Roberto Bueso

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Bowing to praise in San Sebastián Competition and sold by Film Factory, ‘El Mal Padre’ stars Eduard Fernández, a National Cinematography Award winner in Spain

Roberto Bueso‘s “El Mal Padre” (“The Bad Father”), a San Sebastian main competition contender sold by Film Factory, marks a tragicomic take on the mistakes of parenthood, and one son’s struggle not to repeat them.

Eduard Fernández (“Marco,” 2024), a four-time Goya winner and recent recipient of Spain’s National Cinematography Award, plays Santiago Balmes, a celebrated writer and academic who, in the face of a terminal diagnosis, calls together his estranged adult children with the hope of healing. Related Stories UFC 333 Livestream: Here's How to Watch Volkanovski vs. Evloev Online for Free

What emerges is a portrait of a stubborn father trying to make amends, and a quiet reckoning with how futile that effort can be when it comes too late. Fernández leads a cast that includes Adam Jezierski (“Vota Juan,” 2019), Francesco Carril (“The New Years,” 2024), and Meritxell Calvo (“Burning Body,” 2023).

Bueso co-wrote the film with Óscar Díaz Cruz. It is his third feature after “La Banda” (2019) and “Llenos de Gracia” (2022), both of which played Spain’s Málaga Festival, and marked his first time in San Sebastián‘s main competition.

“El Mal Padre” is produced by Fernando Bovaira‘s MOD Producciones and MOD Pictures alongside Misent Producciones and Atresmedia Cine, with the participation of Atresmedia, Netflix and Valencian public broadcaster À Punt, and financing from Spain’s ICAA.

Alex Catalán served as cinematographer. Caramel Films will release the film in Spanish theaters on Nov. 20.

Variety spoke with Bueso about “El Mal Padre.”

This film was born out of losing your father and becoming one yourself. How did making the film help you process these difficult life changes?

It has been a very positive therapeutic process. Starting from my own experience gives you a solid foundation to work with conviction and honesty. Óscar Díaz Cruz, my writing partner, also became a father for the first time while we were looking for the story. We realized that the questions we were asking ourselves had to do with the fear of facing fatherhood. Our generation has reflected on and come to realize that many of the ideas held by the previous generation about the role of men, and therefore of fathers, can no longer be applied to ours. We wanted to try to bring those ideas into dialogue.

Eduard Fernández has talked about how hard it was to play someone this unlikeable. What did you two decide was the line for the character?

We never talked about specific red lines. We wanted the character to be very emotionally clumsy. He had made a lot of mistakes on an emotional level, causing a great deal of pain around him, but he still tried to justify himself. We didn’t want to judge the character. For Eduard and me, it was important to maintain the character’s harshness, while also giving him charisma and a sense of humor. We wanted to show his mistakes and contradictions, but also give him the opportunity to show his vulnerability and honestly reflect on the damage he had caused the people around him. It was a beautiful process, searching for that balance together with Eduard.

In terms of scale, “El Mal Padre” is a more intimate film than your first two. How have you found the shift in production?

This film has ended up being closer to me. I’m very fond of my first two films, but I think that with this third one I’ve been able to go deeper into my own vision and sensibility. Every project is shaped by the circumstances in which it comes to life. With this film, I’ve dealt with themes that were closer to me and that involved greater maturity. In any case, I always try to be honest about what I’m telling.

The film was shot entirely in the Comunidad Valenciana. Why was filming there important to the tone and story?

I’m from Valencia, and I know my homeland very well. Its landscapes are part of my emotional memory. I remember my childhood summers with a particular kind of light, with the presence of the sea and the rocks. For me, it has been a privilege to share that childhood Arcadia with the characters in the film, especially the children. I wanted them to return to the place where their family was still together before everything fell apart, and for it to be precisely there that they would have the opportunity to recreate a similar image, even if only for a moment.

→ Baca artikel asli

Prime Video, RTP Take Portuguese Rights to ‘Gisberta,’ Leonel Vieira’s True-Crime Docuseries

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The four-part Portugal-Brazil co-production, directed by Vieira and Rodrigo Rebouças, marks Volf Entertainment’s first true-crime series

Prime Video and Portuguese public broadcaster RTP have acquired rights in Portugal to “Gisberta – Life and Death of a Trans Woman,” a four-part true-crime docuseries from Leonel Vieira, the director-producer behind box office hit “O Pátio das Cantigas” and HBO Max thriller “O Grito.”

The four 45-minute episodes are in post-production at Lisbon-based Volf Entertainment, which is producing with Brazil’s Ultravioleta. Prime Video has SVOD rights in Portugal; RTP holds free-to-air and AVOD rights. Related Stories Starz Beats Wall Street's Q3 Earnings Estimates as Net Loss Narrows to $35 Million, Linear TV Revenue Dips Byron Allen Earns Profit After Selling All the Starz Stock He Bought From Steven Mnuchin

Brazilian filmmaker Rodrigo Rebouças (“Meninos ao Mar”) created the project and co-directs with Vieira, who also serves as showrunner.

“Gisberta” revisits the 2006 murder of Gisberta Salce Júnior, a Brazilian transgender woman killed in Porto after being tortured by 14 minors from the Catholic-run Oficina de São José. The teenagers were acquitted of homicide, although some received lesser penalties or educational measures. The case sparked outrage in Portugal and Brazil.

Drawing on archive footage, court records, Gisberta’s letters, interviews and dramatized reconstructions, the series traces her life from Brazil to Europe, the killing and its judicial aftermath. It also examines allegations of sexual abuse and mistreatment of children at the institution, which closed in 2010. Interviewees include relatives, friends, lawyers, journalists and people connected to the case.

The project was presented at Conecta Fiction & Entertainment in Toledo in 2024, where it won two awards: Acorde music-licensing support and an invitation to Chile’s Sanfic Industria Series Lab and pitching sessions.

“The Conecta awards gave the project a good boost: they were the springboard for us to pitch it to Amazon Prime,” Vieira told Variety. “With most of the financing secured between Amazon and the Portuguese cash rebate, we then approached the public broadcaster RTP, which also decided to come on board.”

Vieira is also looking to French distributor About Premium Content (APC), involved in financing the project during development, for international sales.

“APC is an excellent international distributor for this project, and we’re confident they will do a great job with it,” he said.

Founded in 2022 and backed by an investment fund managed by Quadrantis Capital, Volf has built its slate around Vieira’s extensive film and television career. “Gisberta” marks the company’s first move into true crime.

“Yes, it is Volf’s first true-crime series and, I believe, the first to be produced for a streaming platform in Portugal,” Vieira said. “We hope it does well, because we have another powerful story that we could also tell.”

Volf’s credits also span the Spain-Portugal thriller “Favàritx,” released on Max and RTP. Vieira is in post-production on “A Noite” (“The Night”), his José Saramago adaptation co-produced with Spain’s Spal Films and starring Nuno Lopes, Adriano Luz and Enrique Arce.

‘Gatto’ Trailer: A Mobster Cat Is on His Last Life in Pixar’s New Animated Movie

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Pixar has let the cat out of the bag with a new trailer for its the upcoming original animated feature “Gatto,” about the scrappy black feline Nero who lives in Venice, Italy.

“Avengers” star Mark Ruffalo voices Nero, who is indebted to local feline mob boss Rocco, voiced by “Matrix” star Laurence Fishburne. The story revolves around Nero, who has been questioning whether he’s lived the right lives in the city of canals, and is forced to forge an unexpected friendship that may finally lead him to his purpose, according to the official synopsis. Related Stories UFC 333 Livestream: Here's How to Watch Volkanovski vs. Evloev Online for Free

The cast includes Bianca Babaşa as Maja; Sherry Cola as Regina; John Turturro as Capitano Briganti; Rumen Sali Shopov as Papu Janko; Rhys Darby as Focaccia; Isabella Rossellini as Contessa; Natasia Demetriou as Charon; Jamie Demetriou as Plague Doctor; Jason Schwartzman as Vivaldi; Saverio Raimondo as Ugo; Jason Mantzoukas as Rico; Matt Jones as Enzo; Austin Madison as Fishmonger; Beatrice Manfredi as Martinelli; Jesse Andrews as Bartolomeo; Tracey Ullman as Luisa; and Marco Calvani as Maestro Foscari.

Directed by Enrico Casarosa, “Gatto” is produced by Andrea Warren in a follow-up to the pair’s Oscar-nominated feature “Luca.”

49ers pivoted to Keenan Allen trade after initially focusing on Josh Downs

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The 49ers made a trade for wide receiver Keenan Allen on Tuesday, but he wasn’t the only Colts wide receiver on their radar ahead of the trade deadline.

During a Thursday appearance on KNBR, General Manager John Lynch said that the team turned its attention to Allen after initially targeting Josh Downs as an addition to their receiver room. Lynch said that head coach Kyle Shanahan brought up Allen while watching film on Downs and conversations with the Colts began before they landed defensive tackle DeForest Buckner in an earlier deal with Indianapolis.

“We kind of focused on Josh Downs as an option that we’d have some interest,” Lynch said, via 49ersWebzone.com. “And Kyle and I are kind of watching Josh, and in the process, Kyle’s like, ‘Man, what about Keenan?’ And I was like, ‘You know, we hadn’t given much thought to that, but let me ask about that.’ And so, there started a conversation about two and a half weeks ago, really, when the DeForest talks [happened]. ‘Would he?’ And it’s always fluid, and at a certain time, they didn’t want to give up Keenan. It kind of wasn’t going to be on the table, but we kept communicating, and ultimately we got him.”

The 49ers sent a 2027 sixth-round pick to the Colts for Allen. Downs and a 2027 seventh-round pick were eventually traded to the Chiefs for 2027 fourth- and fifth-round picks.

Scott Disick Makes Rare Comments About Raising Mason Disick

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Scott Disick shared rare insight into parenting his and ex Kourtney Kardashian's 16-year-old son, admitting that it's "scarier" than raising Penelope, 14, and Reign, 11.

Scott Disick is having a hard time keeping up with his teen.

The Kardashians star shared insight into how parenting his and ex Kourtney Kardashian's 16-year-old son Mason compares to raising his younger siblings Penelope, 14, and Reign, 11.

"I kind of consider Reign a little kid still 'cause I got Mace who's like an adult," Scott explained to Khloe Kardashian during the Nov. 12 Kardashians episode. "It's so much scarier."

"For instance, this past weekend, they were up all night out, went to some parties," he continued, "and I literally just sat on my phone and looked at Life360 and Find My app to see where Mace was until he got home—the whole night."

Meanwhile, Khloe—mom to kids True, 8, and Tatum, 4, with ex Tristan Thompson—related to Scott's concerns about Mason, already worrying about her kids getting older.

"I will 100 percent track my kids," the Khloe in Wonder Land podcast host admitted during a confessional. "I would put a chip in my children to know where they are at all times."

"If it's safe, that's what I have to look into," she teased. "Why is this not suggested more? So, I'm a believer in things that maybe other people are not."

As for Scott, he and Kourtney—whom he dated on and off from 2006 to 2015—are partners in navigating their children through their teenage years. And even though the pair ultimately decided they were better suited to a platonic relationship, they still had to get their f--king asses up and work on their co-parenting.

As Kourtney previously admitted, "It's not easy," with the Poosh founder adding during her October 2025 confessional on The Kardashians, "I think, with co-parenting, it's always trying to be cordial, do our best to be on the same page."

Meanwhile, Scott has discussed how his bond with Kourtney—who also shares Rocky, 3, as well as stepchildren Atiana De La Hoya, 27, Landon Barker, 23, and Alabama Barker, 20, with husband Travis Barker—has been "one of the more difficult things" in his life.

"I always had her friends or our family in my ear kind of like, 'Don't worry, honey, eventually you guys will figure it out,'" he said during a 2022 episode of the reality show. "And, 'You'll get back together one day.' So my foot was always halfway in the door."

Though Scott is happy that Kourtney is doing amazing with her sweetie, whom she wed in 2022, he admitted that he was especially afraid of losing his relationship with the entire family.

"Feeling left out and not being told anything is super hurtful," he shared, noting, however, that when it came to seeing Travis and Kourtney together romantically, "I'd rather be around them and be around my family than not at all."

The animal welfare nonprofit shared a photo of Swift and Kelce with Kelce’s longtime agent, Mike Simon, and his wife Lisa Marie, who attended their wedding. ‘Harry Potter’s Best Spin-Off Is Officially a Streaming Hit Before HBO’s Reboot

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fifteen years after the final Harry Potter movie, and just over a month before HBO's reboot arrives on December 25, the world still has room for more magic. Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking has become a fan-favorite addition to the Wizarding World of Harry Potter, bringing the beloved franchise back to screens with a baking competition on Food Network.

The show is graced with guest judges who are actors and actresses from the movie franchise that any fan will recognize immediately. The beloved story is a draw for the audience and the bakers, who all take great care in producing the most spectacular pieces of art out of pastries and candy. It is clear how much thought each baker puts into their creations and the flavors they bring to the table. Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking is a charming show that has brought magic to Food Network in a way that fans will surely enjoy.

Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking focuses on baking while weaving in the Harry Potter franchise audiences know and love. The show is hosted by not one, but two actors from the Harry Potter films, James Phelps and Oliver Phelps, who play the Weasley twins in all eight movies. The first season introduces nine teams, sent through the fireplace via the flue network, who are paired together based on their shared love for one specific part of Harry Potter. The teams get to work immediately with their first task: a two-foot-tall sculpture of a scene or set piece from the film. Ultimately, only six teams got on the Hogwarts Express and advanced to the next episode.

Even with baking being such a large part of the show, it is centered around the Wizarding World. James and Oliver Phelps do a marvelous job of hosting with funny quips and references. Notably, the Phelps twins are not judging the competition but get the chance to snack on the treats being made, even being given an entire tray of leftover mango caramel. In Season 1, Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking was judged by Carla Hall, a Food Network regular, and Jozef Youssef, an award-winning chef. Joining the pair of highly acclaimed chefs are celebrity cameos such as Bonnie Wright, Evanna Lynch, and Warwick Davis – who play Ginny Weasley, Luna Lovegood, and Professor Flitwick, respectively.

Four worlds and four completely different ideas of what a good life looks like. This is where you belong, what it would ask of you, and what you would get back in return.

You belong somewhere old enough that the mountains have names and somebody can still tell you who gave them. Middle-earth is for people who think the right thing is worth doing even when it will not be finished in their lifetime.

You want the sea, a crew, and absolutely nobody telling you where you are allowed to go. The Grand Line is the world for people whose main ambition in life is finding out what is over there.

You would rather be feared and useful than liked and useless. The Continent is a world where the monsters are the straightforward part of the job and the people are where it all gets complicated.

You want the myths to be real and loose in the actual world, and you want to find out at about thirteen that you were never anywhere near as ordinary as everybody kept telling you.

The guest stars are not the only thing Harry Potter fans can expect from Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking. There are references to the Harry Potter films everywhere. Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking is shot at Warner Bros. Studios and has pieces from the film, from the Hogwarts Express to a portrait of the Fat Lady that hangs over the fireplace. With clips of the films cut in between each reference to remind the audience of what some scenes looked like, and each challenge being something wonderful and beloved from the film, there is no forgetting that Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking is for the fans.

The series is now in its third season, which premiered November 4 with a Hogwarts House tournament and new judges Duff Goldman and Sarah Mountain. Per FlixPatrol for the Week of November 9, Wizards of Baking ranked No. 5 on HBO Max's U.S. Top 10 TV Shows chart, giving fans another way to revisit the Wizarding World before the December reboot.

From the beginning, it is clear that this is a baking show, so although knowledge and love of the Harry Potter franchise are massive benefits to the bakers, the taste and creativity will move them further in the competition. The nine baking teams get to show off their knowledge of Harry Potter and baking skills with themed challenges. Moving parts are in many of the pieces, including lights, chocolate fountains, spiders on tracks, and messages hidden in clear candy.

Each team can show off two tasting portions, one for each baker (several of whom appeared on Is It Cake? on Netflix). The teams fight to keep their spot in the competition and learn to work with each other in the kitchen. The show's charm is the baking and the teams' apparent care when creating each piece.

The magic and beauty of Hogwarts have come to Food Network with Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking, and it does not disappoint. Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking is delightful, with James and Oliver Phelps flourishing as hosts and joined by their co-stars. The baking is also spectacular, and it is evident from the intricacies and detail how much care goes into crafting each confection. Incorporating one of the most beloved franchises with a cooking competition was an excellent move for Food Network and has produced a fun and charming show that any fan will love. If food and fandom are what you're looking for, Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking takes the cake.

Harry Potter: Wizards of Baking airs weekly on Food Network and can be streamed on HBO Max with a subscription.

‘The Empress’ Delivers a Satisfying Ending for One of Netflix’s Best Hidden Gems | Review

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

German period drama The Empress is one of Netflix’s most underrated gems, a historical series that dramatizes the story of Elisabeth I of Austria (Devrim Lingnau Islamoğlu), the daughter of a Bavarian duke who married an emperor for love and became famous the world over for her beauty and independent spirit. Set not in Tudor or Regency England—as so many of these sorts of series often are— but in 19th-century Austria, the story blends fairytale romance with political conflict and social intrigue as Elisabeth and her husband, the Emperor Franz Joseph (Philip Froissant), attempt to navigate both threats to their throne and the stifling responsibilities of power.

Much like the historical period it’s set in, the show’s third and final season takes on a darker and increasingly tense tone. Struggles for independence in various crown lands spark war, political upheaval, and questions about what the future of the Habsburg Empire can and should look like. The fate of one marriage is perhaps a small thing next to the intensity of international geopolitics, but it is Franz and Elisabeth’s relationship that remains the heart of The Empress until its final frames.

The final season picks up a few months after the dramatic conclusion of Season 2, which saw Franz leave to command his army on the front lines of the Sardinian War. Elisabeth, who has finally managed to give birth to the male heir that secures the Habsburg family succession, is left behind in Vienna, alongside her difficult mother-in-law, the Archduchess Sophie (Melika Foroutan). But while Franz ultimately makes it back from the fighting safely, the emperor who returns from the front is a very different man from the one who left.

Traumatized by all the death he has both witnessed and been responsible for, he’s rude and standoffish, repeatedly pushing away his family and rejecting the comforting advances of his wife. Froissant does some of his best work in these early episodes focused on Franz’s return, where it's not only clear how much he’s hurting, but how ill-equipped he is to deal with the mental and emotional fallout from a war he chose to fight.

Only through his affair with an unassuming woman named Terese (Marlene Burow) does Franz begin to find something like peace again, though that decision ultimately costs him in other areas of his life. It’s a dark period for both the Austrian royals: the discovery of her husband’s infidelity, along with a debilitating health scare, sends Elisabeth out of Vienna—and, more importantly, away from her husband and his mother—for the first time since her marriage.

The real Elisabeth loved to travel, and this shift is an important part of the character’s understanding of both herself and her relationship to the larger world. Lingnau is luminous as a young woman restored to the freedom that has long been denied her, and she effortlessly conveys determination to do right by the people who have entrusted her with power over them. On her journey home from the picturesque island of Madeira, she meets the dashing Count Andrássy (Dejan Bućin), a mysterious Hungarian revolutionary. Their intense connection happens almost laughably quickly–-over the course of what is maybe two conversations—but his charismatic personality draws her swiftly into his country’s political fight for self-determination, even though it frequently puts her at odds with Franz and Sophie’s ideas about Austria’s future.

Still, despite all the political wrangling between empires, vassal states looking for the chance to determine their own destinies, and Habsburg sibling Maximilian’s (Johannes Nussbaum) ascending to the throne of Mexico, the chemistry between Lingnau and Froissant remains the emotional glue that holds this series together. Sure, Franz and Elisabeth struggle with both marital and political problems, but no matter what happens, they still can’t seem to give up on one another.

Behind the scenes of 'The Empress', Netflix’s popular historical drama - from the sets and filming locations to costumes, stunts, and the approach the cast took to portraying this fascinating chapter in history.

Foroutan pulls double duty this season as both Archduchess Sophie and her twin sister, Princess Maria Anna of Bavaria, who appears just long enough to ruin a key political negotiation with Kaiser Wilhelm II (Sebastian Blomberg). And Nussbaum gets to play Maximillian at both his most idealistic and utterly defeated. While his character has long been notable for being... let’s just call it performatively over the top, this is perhaps the most human version of him we’ve ever seen. Also, it should be noted that costume designer Gabriela Reumer once again knocks it out of the park, crafting a stunning series of sumptuous gowns and lavish uniforms that serve as various forms of armor for almost all of the show’s characters.

In keeping with its overall fairytale feel, The Empress’ ending blends both fiction and fact. The show simply doesn’t mention some of the more harrowing events of Elisabeth’s later life and reign, glossing over the eating disorder-like obsession with her own weight and leaving out the tragic loss of her only son Rudolf, who was found dead with his mistress in what was suspected to be a murder-suicide. Her assassination at the hands of an Italian anarchist is reduced to a footnote that appears onscreen before the finale’s closing credits.

Instead, The Empress turns the establishment of the Austro-Hungarian Monarchy into a turning point for many things — a new dawn for independence in Europe and a fresh start for Franz and Elisabeth’s relationship, grounded in an understanding that this particular empress needs the freedom to be herself. Is it an ending that's particularly truthful about the events of these people’s lives and their relationships with one another? Not really. But is it a satisfying (if poignant) conclusion to the story we’ve spent three seasons watching? Absolutely. In a season festooned with so much darkness, it offers a welcome burst of hope that maybe, sometimes, love really can conquer all.

The Empress Season 3 is now streaming on Netflix.

A satisfying, poignant conclusion for one of Netflix's best hidden gems.
